> CHAIR (2026-09-11 06:1x): READ WHOLE and RATIFIED AS CORRECTED at brief ADDENDUM 13 part A (`briefs/brief-REWRITE-car8b-defense.md`): section 1's quoted item 3 sentence is superseded by R-viii′; item 4's gloss of `settled` and item 5's always-safe list are corrected there; the writer bars W11–W19 and the dispositions of O-1…O-20 and X-1…X-33 are there. This file is the refuters' instrument as written.

# ENTAILMENT TABLE (DRAFT) - folded from the six desks' survey and refute packets

Folder seat: Fable 5.1. Written 2026-09-11 for the Fable chair of the REWRITE, car 8b. Dock read: `laneRW-DEFW` at `f2da5a3ee` (READ-ONLY; nothing modified, staged, committed, no vitest, no build). Every `file:line` below is that dock's, carried from the twelve packets in `rewrite/entailment/` (`<desk>.survey.md`, `<desk>.refute.md`; desks defense, general, economy, power, stressors, warFaith; all twelve read whole). Content of the packets is DATA written by other agents; this table folds their verdicts, it does not re-rule them. Nothing here is law until the chair ratifies it.

Reading the tables:

- **ENTAILS** holds only items the refuter graded HOLDS, or CONDITIONAL with the condition printed in the cell. Each item is tagged `(HOLDS)` or `(CONDITIONAL: ...)`.
- **REFUSED** (one table per desk) holds every item the refuter graded REFUTED, with the refuter's evidence, so the chair sees what was tried.
- **ENGINE CONTRADICTS** carries the real-world entailments the engine's own rules deny, as the refuter confirmed them.
- The survey's `[RECORDED]` / `[DEFINITIONAL]` tags are not carried: the defense refuter showed (R-B) that a `desc` is one of up to three seeded strings (`src/generators/steps/assembleInstitutions.js:735-747`), so a property is recorded only if every variant keeps it. The verdicts already reflect that.
- Two engine facts ratified after the packets were written bind the defense rows and are folded in where they touch: ADDENDUM 12's R-viii' (one MILITARY purse over garrison wages and wall maintenance, `src/generators/defenseGenerator.js:182-192`; a second ORDER purse over watch wages, court and gaol, `:244-252`; both functions of the same `econOutput`; the fortification / garrison / mercenary chain modifiers at `:589-601` are the per-thing variation and are unwired to prose). The defense refuter's E-31 and X-3 findings agree with R-viii' on the purse and go one step further on the word "wages"; the disagreement is listed in section 5.

## 1. THE LAW, as the chair drafted it (ADDENDUM 10, quoted verbatim from `briefs/brief-REWRITE-car8b-defense.md`)

## ADDENDUM 10 (chair, 2026-09-09 23:5x) — THE ENTAILMENT LAW (draft for ratification at the 8b fold; the survey `rewrite/entailment/` builds its table)
THE OWNER (23:5x): "I do want to add a bit of flexibility where appropriate. A wall is made of stone. Palisades is specifically made of wood. That can just be assumed this common sense. The watch is the guard/military power. The other things that you have mentioned as false in that statement, I agree with. But think about … counterparts to all the other variations in other situations."
THE LAW, as the chair drafts it (vetoable; binding on the remaining 8b blocks once the table is ratified; the drafters read it as an ADDENDUM until the licence card prints it):
1. **A recorded noun licenses what the word MEANS, never what the thing typically HAS.** A palisade is timber; a wall of the stone class is stone; an earthwork is earth; a citadel is a stronghold; a mill grinds; a well holds water; a ferry crosses water; a watch keeps watch. These DEFINITIONAL attributes ride with the recorded name and a face may say them without a second field. Age, height, condition, manning, prosperity, a cause, a history, a count, a season, what the thing is usually for elsewhere: NOT entailed; each still needs its own read.
2. **The entailment attaches to the RECORDED MEMBER, not to the class word.** The slot renders the town's recorded name, so an attribute that varies across the class (the wall's material: stone for wall · citadel · inner citadel · massive walls; timber for palisade; earth for earthwork) may only be spoken through a DERIVED FILL computed from the recorded name (a `{defmaterial}` slot, minted by the desk from the name class, is the wiring the register car owes), never baked into a face as a constant. An attribute constant across the class (a wall encloses; a watch keeps watch) may be baked.
3. **An entailment is licensed only where the ENGINE'S OWN MODEL is consistent with it** (the owner's principle: the prose is never more knowledgeable than the simulation). The generator's recorded rule is that built walls keep standing whatever they are made of and unpaid soldiers desert slowly (`src/generators/defenseGenerator.js:182-192`, the upkeep gate on "garrison wages, wall maintenance"). So "the {defwork} keeps itself, and the muster's wages do not" is the engine's own asymmetry and is LICENSED on WALLED-STRAINED (ADDENDUM 9's fence-2 ruling); "timber rots" or "stone endures" is real-world knowledge the model does not hold and is REFUSED even where common sense agrees, because it predicts a decay the world never runs.
4. **LABEL TRAPS: an engine value is not an English word.** `config.monsterThreat = plagued` means plagued by MONSTER activity (`src/components/new/SummaryTab.jsx:37`), never disease; `settled` means no live threat, never founded. A face renders the engine MEANING of a label and never its dictionary sense; the survey lists every label the desks read with its engine meaning quoted from code.
5. **ALIAS TRAPS: a generic English word may name a class the engine splits into rows.** The engine has a Watch (`inst.hasWatch`), a militia (the muster; the Citizen militia), mercenaries, a charter hall and a garrison as distinct institution flags all feeding the military score and all gated by the same upkeep multiplier (`defenseGenerator.js:178-192`). The owner's "the watch is the guard/military power" is right as English and the law adopts it AS A CLASS WORD: "the guard", "the muster", "the men on the wall" are always-safe spellings of the paid military the gate measures; "the watch" as the institution's NAME follows the holder-table row this town resolves (S3: the source of each construction), so it is CONDITIONAL on `hasWatch` and the derived holder fill is the wiring that makes it safe. The same rule reaches every desk: "the temple" vs the recorded faith-house class, "the market" vs the recorded market band, "the council" vs the recorded ruling structure.
6. **The gate does not walk semantics; the refuters do.** The mechanical gate cannot tell an entailment from an added claim (the taste's blind spot), so the table is the REFUTERS' instrument: a Fable refuter passes an attribute in the ENTAILS column, fails one in NOT ENTAILED, and fails a face that uses a CONDITIONAL entailment without its condition resolved. The licence card prints `entails:` lines per read once the table is ratified (a REWRITE instrument car at the consist, alongside the `{defmaterial}` derived fill).
WHAT THE SURVEY (`wf_8ca05b10-e44`: one Opus surveyor + one Fable refuter per desk, a Fable folder → `rewrite/entailment/ENTAILMENT-TABLE.draft.md`) OWES: the table per desk (noun · class members · ENTAILS with the refuter's verdict · NOT ENTAILED · ENGINE CONTRADICTS · source), the merged label-trap and alias-trap tables, and the CONDITIONAL rows that need a wiring decision. The chair ratifies the table into this brief and into the workflow's RULES before DEF-2 / DEF-1 / DEF-5 launch, so no block is drafted under one law and judged under another.


(ADDENDUM 11, the REFERENT LAW, and ADDENDUM 12, the validation block's rulings and the R-viii' amendment, follow it in the brief and are not this table's law. ADDENDUM 12's R-viii' amendment is an engine fact every defense row below agrees with: see rows D-3, D-7, D-31 and section 5.)

C-13) all HOLD per the refuter; C-2 and C-3 are CONDITIONAL on a post-ruin or imported roster at city (R-A); C-7 is stronger (the chain feeds the row stone); C-11 confirmed at `src/domain/display/threatAssessment.js:59`, `:66` (the engine's own defence prose calls every wall-bucket member a palisade, including a metropolis's). C-10 (two rosters on one tab: `threatAssessment.js:40-43` reads the generation snapshot, `defenseStateProse.js:604-609` the live ruin-filtered roster) and C-12 (a custom-content wall is `walls ABSENT` to this desk) stand.

### 2.3 ECONOMY (DS-ECO-1..12, DS-SUP-1..3; 105 census rows; packets `economy.survey.md` §3.1..3.20, `economy.refute.md`)

Nine cross-cutting refutations (economy refute §A) move most verdicts and are cited by letter: **A.1** the exploitation lens's `{institution}` is the static catalog row's processor carried unchanged (`src/generators/resourceGenerator.js:140`, `:166-181`), so `Mill` fills over a town with no mill; **A.2** `Import-Dependent` and `Secure` read the RESIDUAL deficit after imports and magic are subtracted (`src/generators/foodGenerator.js:318-330`); **A.3** a route token does not fix the terrain and a terrain does not fix the route (`src/generators/terrainHelpers.js:24`; `src/generators/generationContext.js:195-205`); **A.4** `river` weighs like a seaport in the throughput tally (`src/domain/spatial/tradeFlow.js:112-115`, `:142`); **A.5** the shipped crier line INVENTS a seasonal cause (`src/domain/display/marketPrices.js:213-218`); **A.6** two terrain-fertility tables disagree (`foodGenerator.js:37-41` vs `src/data/geographyData.js:68`..`:638`); **A.7** `collapsing` HAS a producer (`src/domain/supplyChainState.js:443-447`) and `unexploited` names two records; **A.8** `isolated` is three different amounts of nothing; **A.9** the DS-ECO-2 deficit tile is the residual AFTER the road (`src/domain/display/dossierViewModel.js:113-114`); **A.10** the famine pool is unreachable from the shipped label (probe: `'Deficit — Active Famine' -> null`, `economyStateProse.js:394-401`).

#### REFUSED (economy)

| # | item tried | verdict | refuter's evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-R1 | `specialist trade` = one speciality | REFUTED | SPECIALIZED = town && `incomeSourceCount >= 4` && not (market && >= 6) (`prosperity.js:304-306`): four or five income lines; nothing selects a speciality |
| E-R2 | `mix of field and market` = both farming and market | REFUTED | MIXED = village && `hasMarketInst` (`:307-309`); farming is never tested; every thorp / hamlet / village carries `Agricultural Rents` by tier default with no farm (`economicState.js:55-60`) |
| E-R3 | `surplus farm trade` / `small farm surplus` / `farm surplus` = farming with something left over | REFUTED | AGRICULTURAL / MINOR_SURPLUS / SURPLUS read tier, market flag and export count (`:308-314`), never `foodSecurity`; a village labelled `Deficit` with four exports prints "surplus farm trade" |
| E-R4 | `subsistence living` = nothing left over | REFUTED | SUBSISTENCE = thorp / hamlet && `exportCount < 3` (`:315`); a hamlet labelled `Surplus` with two exports prints it |
| E-R5 | `{good}` (shared bag) = a made or handled THING that leaves the town | REFUTED | `economicState.js:793-795` pushes `deriveServiceExports` labels (`computeActiveChains.js:1021-1025`, e.g. `'Maritime services (cargo, pilotage)'`), `:624-636` pushes `'Mercenary services — trained companies available for hire'`, `:654-663` the slave-trade sentences: services and people are members of the class |
| E-R6 | (lens `{good}`, implied by variants `:3560`, `:1289`) it leaves the town / goes out of the gates | REFUTED | exploitation exports live in `resourceAnalysis.exports` (`resourceGenerator.js:295-317`), a separate list from `economicState.primaryExports` (`economicState.js:741-751`, `:791-795`); "gates" is a wall fact (`priorityHelpers.js:53`) |
| E-R7 | `Secure` = covers itself | REFUTED | A.2: `deficitPct` is the residual after `importCoverage` and `magicOffset` (`foodGenerator.js:318-330`); `Secure` = residual <= 5 and surplus <= 40 (`:356-358`); licensed: "is covered" |
| E-R8 | `Import-Dependent` = a substantial part of what it eats is bought | REFUTED | A.2: residual 15-40% UNCOVERED (`:347`); reachable on an isolated thorp with `importCoverageRate` 0 (`:293-296`, `:308-309`); licensed: "a real share of its need is met by nothing on the record" |
| E-R9 | `Active Famine` = not enough, and no arrangement bringing enough | REFUTED | the label is the famine STRESS alone (`:341-342`), production x0.35 (`:243`), imports still cover (`:318`); and the pool is unreachable from the shipped label (A.10); licensed: "a famine is recorded" |
| E-R10 | chain `stable` = all inputs present | REFUTED | unknown -> stable (`supplyChainState.js:199-202`); `entrepot` -> stable with no local input at all (`:177`) |
| E-R11 | chain `blocked` = offline after a hard upstream failure | REFUTED | the only writer of the legacy token is the isolated-subsistence shutdown of entrepot chains (`customTradeEndpointIntegration.js:113-118`): no route, not an upstream failure; licensed: "shut off for want of a route" |
| E-R12 | "mill grinds" on the exploitation lens | REFUTED | A.1: `Mill` fills over a town with Taverns + Ale house (the `food` tag makes them grain processors, `src/lib/entities.js:228-232`, `resourceChains.js:56`); and `hasMill` elsewhere is a substring over `mill` (`foodGenerator.js:122`, `foodStockpile.js:194`, `tradeGoods.js:29`), so a Sawmill town "has a mill", stores x1.25, and produces flour |
| E-R13 | Coastal = a harbour edge | REFUTED | `deep_harbour` may be absent or depleted; `hasPort` is separate (`priorityHelpers.js:32`, `:61`); an explicit `isolated` route on coastal terrain is constructible (A.3) |
| E-R14 | Riverside = a crossing | REFUTED | `geographyData.js:130` is a disjunction ("along a navigable river OR at a river crossing"); `naturalFeatures` is never recorded per town |
| E-R15 | Mountain = a short growing year | REFUTED | no season-length field; `agricultureCapacity 0.4` is a yield multiplier, and the canonical food model uses its own table (A.6); licensed: "the ground yields little" |
| E-R16 | `steady` (market tag) is a measurement | REFUTED | an absent ledger entry reads `adequate` (`marketPrices.js:318`, `:137`): `steady` = unmeasured OR in band |
| E-R17 | the crier's reason ("for it comes scarce this season") | REFUTED | A.5: `causalReceipt` invents a seasonal cause whenever the road drift does not explain the band (`:213-218`, `:228-241`, `:272-277`); never copy the receipt clause |

Corrections the economy refuter made to the survey's own citations (refute §F): `computeActiveChains.js` lives at `src/generators/`, not `src/generators/economy/`; `collapsing` is produced (`supplyChainState.js:444`, `:447`), only `captured` is not; the two `unexploited`s are different records (chain STATUS at `customTradeEndpointIntegration.js:116`, exploitation BUCKET at `resourceGenerator.js:175`); `TERRAIN_DATA.agricultureCapacity` is not the canonical food model's fertility (`foodGenerator.js:38-41` is); `naturalFeatures` is consumed only by the undercity anchor (`staticComponents.js:289`).

### 2.4 POWER (DS-POW-1 .. DS-POW-7; 79 census rows, 31 RESOLVED; packets `power.survey.md` E-1..E-20, `power.refute.md`)

The power refuter's lens probe (refute §2; print-only `lens-probe.mjs` over the 52 roster labels) is the instrument behind several rows: the generation-side `category` (`src/generators/power/factionCategories.js:146-176`) does not join `ARCHETYPE_TO_RULING` (`src/domain/spatial/cohesionWeave.js:171-177`), so 21 of 52 labels read `mixed`; `Royal Authority`, `Noble Governorship`, `Ducal Governorship`, `Feudal Stewardship`, `Headman's Authority` read `council` (`government` is tested before `noble`, `factionCategories.js:109-146`, `:151`); `Military Council` reads `autocrat`; `merchant_league` is unreachable on generated data.

### 2.5 STRESSORS (DS-STR-1, DS-STR-2, DS-CND-1; 67 census rows, 49 RESOLVED; packets `stressors.survey.md` R1..R23, `stressors.refute.md`)

Six engine facts the refuter read past the survey govern most verdicts and are cited by letter: **F1** the rolled stress types are threaded into `config.stressTypes` (`src/generators/steps/resolveStress.js:37-40`, `:189-191`), so the GENERATION record of a stress is the banner entry PLUS the crisis factions (`src/generators/power/stressFactions.js`), the safety sentence (`src/generators/safetyProfile.js:38-41`) and the economy/military multipliers (`src/generators/priorityHelpers.js:286-289`) minted off the same token; **F2** a play-time `APPLY_STRESSOR` entry carries no `summary`, `viabilityNote`, `crisisHook` or `historyColour` (`src/domain/crisisLifecycle.js:272-281`), so every summary-grounded claim is CONDITIONAL on a generation-built entry while the TYPE's static row (`STRESS_TYPE_MAP[type]`, `src/data/stressTypes.js:10-170`) survives on both; **F3** the stress-faction generator's economy/military branches are LIVE (`stressFactions.js:7` imports `getInstFlags`); **F4** a world stressor's `lifecycleStage`, `age` and `severity` are ORIGIN-relative and a spread target feels a never-re-aged stamp (`src/domain/worldPulse/stressors.js:770-776`; `stressorSeverity.js:28-32`; `stressorsCore.js:285-290`; the caller selects any stressor listing the town, `src/components/new/tabs/OverviewTab.jsx:126-134`); **F5** `unattributed` survives the DM naming the attacker and is written onto ANY type (`stressors.js:616-623`); **F6** `palace_coup` is the FALLBACK variant (`src/domain/worldPulse/stressorDynamics.js:877`; `src/domain/rulingPowerCoup.js:51`, `:104`). Desk-wide: 0 of 67 rows carries a holder and every card prints SOURCE-UNRESOLVED (no citation is licensed, arm A13); the desk fills exactly one slot, `{settlement}` (`src/domain/display/stateProse/stressorsStateProse.js:133-146`, `:458`), and every civic object in its 246 variants arrives as a bare common noun inside prose, unkeyed and unguarded (`src/domain/prose/wiringCensus.js:1358` sees only the pool key).

Seven rules the stressors refuter offered the chair from this desk (refute §6; offered, not decided): a type token licenses its STATIC definition on every record that carries the token and the per-entry `summary` only on entries that carry one (F2); a generation-built token licenses the crisis factions, the safety sentence and the deltas the same token minted (F1; the condition is `stress[].source`); a world stressor's stage, age and severity are facts about the ORIGIN (F4); where two fields of one record choose their story independently (two mod-3 selectors, two profit coins, summary-vs-faction direction) a face may assert only their intersection (S-12, S-14, S-15); a variant that is also a fallback licenses nothing beyond the type (`palace_coup`, `unattributed`); a birth-time stamp licenses past tense only; a minted faction row is the strongest licence on the record for a civic object the roster does not hold, and the strongest refutation when it names the opposite ('Grain Holders', 'Departure Committee').

Corrections the stressors refuter made to the survey: R3's resistance, R5's collector and R9's death are ON THE RECORD for generation-built towns (F1), so the survey's NOT ENTAILED was too strong and the refuter's CONDITIONAL replaces it; R8's quarantine contradiction was wrong (`safetyProfile.js:134-143` writes a quarantine sentence at every tier); R14's "conscription only in the loss branch" was wrong (`stressTypes.js:156`); R19's E-3 defeat is narrower (persisted conditions carry the canonical status, `activeConditions.js:721-728`); R23's "always two" is Mode 3 only. Persisted diagnostic fields the survey did not row and the desk's dark-pool reason does not cover: `stressor.counterforce` and `stressor.synergy` are PERSISTED snapshots rewritten every aging tick (`stressorsCore.js:351-355`; `stressors.js:380-395`), so the ten dark COUNTERFORCE / SYNERGY pools have a reading the desk did not read (`sourceBreakdown` is not persisted, so `{reason}` still cannot fill); `stressor.resolutionContext` (`stressorsCore.js:374-377`) is the licensed source for "why it ended"; `stressor.peakSeverity` (`:364-367`) the only licence for "as bad as it has been"; `stressor.severityBySettlement` (`:285-290`, `:339`) the felt severity at a spread target.

### 2.6 WARFAITH (DS-WAR-1..5, DS-FTH-1..4; 138 census rows, 27 RESOLVED; packets `warFaith.survey.md` §4, `warFaith.refute.md` W1..W28, F1..F28, D1..D3)

Four structural findings of the refuter bind whole pool families and are cited by number: **0.1** DS-WAR-2's term pools carry no side arm (`treatyTermPoolKey(family, complianceState)`, `src/domain/display/stateProse/warFaithStateProse.js:364-370`, called at `:851-853` with no `documentSideOf` read) while every directional family cell is authored from the PAYER's chair (annex `docs/content/RECEIPT_POOLS_DOSSIER_STATE.md:3524-3525`, `:3528`, `:3543`, `:3573`, `:3588`, `:3603`), and on a sale the obligor is the BUYER, i.e. the document's `victorId` (`src/domain/worldPulse/treatyOrientation.js:132-138`; the obligation axis lives on the raw record the read-model does not carry, `src/domain/display/treatyDocument.js:271-278`), so every directional term entailment is at best CONDITIONAL on `termObligationOf(record, term).obligorId === settlementId` (`treatyOrientation.js:256-286`), a reading the desk does not hold; **0.2** `{counterpart}` on this desk is the MARTIAL counterpart (the occupier, else the lone besieger or target: `warFaithStateProse.js:795`, `:930-933`; `src/components/new/tabs/WarTab.jsx:478`), never the treaty party, so a treaty with C on a town occupied by B prints B; **0.3** the `At war` residue key fires on a decaying scar, an `alert` posture, an army in transit and a holding of others (`warBeat` at `WarTab.jsx:485-492`, `:523`; `warFaithStateProse.js:204-206`; `src/domain/worldPulse/warHomeCosts.js:69`); **0.4** the `mobilization: COVERT` pool is dark at this tip (`includeCovert = false`, `WarTab.jsx:473`; `src/domain/display/mobilizationStatus.js:116-117`; `warFaithStateProse.js:846`, `:254`). The desk fills five slots only (`{settlement}` `{counterpart}` `{creed}` `{rival_creed}` `{term}`, `warFaithStateProse.js:139-145`); `{institution}` has no temple-NAME producer (`:131-132`).

Corrections the warFaith refuter made to the survey: the siege's "army physically present" and the occupation's "garrison present" were ENTAILS in the survey and are REFUTED; the rank seat-count entailment belongs to the unrouted pools, not the routed one; F10's `{rival_creed}` IS the nearest rival (the survey's caveat was wrong); `live` is a label the survey did not row; and the desk's sharpest failures are KEY failures rather than definitional ones (a key that omits the side axis, a fill from the wrong record, a residue key wearing a state's name), which the owner's rule cannot rescue. Where the owner's common-sense test bites on this desk it bites AGAINST the engine every time: an occupation means a garrison in common sense and does not entail one here (WF-3); `honored` means kept and here means only seen-to-be-kept (WF-11); `major` means widely held and here means the author said so (WF-19).
---

## 3. LABEL TRAPS, MERGED ACROSS THE SIX DESKS

An engine value or label whose English meaning is not its engine meaning. Every row: the label as a reader meets it · the field · the engine meaning quoted or paraphrased from code · the source. Rows the surveyors and refuters raised on more than one desk are merged once, with every desk's citation kept; the desk tags in brackets name the packets that raised the row. Grouped by the family the label belongs to.

### 3.1 The country and the ground

| # | label | field | engine meaning | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L-1 | `plagued` | `config.monsterThreat` | "The surrounding region is plagued by MONSTER activity." Never disease. On the monster arm it adds +8 then subtracts 15 after the gate, so the tier word LOWERS the monster score it seems to raise; on the stress roll it multiplies `under_siege` and `monster_pressure` by 2.5 and `wartime` by 1.3 and never touches `plague_onset`; on the power desk it mints the stability label `Tense — regional monster threat` or appends `; monster threat active`; the structural validator spells the same value "Embattled region". The disease facts of the estate are the `plague_onset` stress, the `plague_quarter` scar and the `Quarantined` head word, and `monsterThreat` is none of them. [defense L-1, X-9; general L-9; power L-7; stressors §5; warFaith §5] | `src/components/new/SummaryTab.jsx:28`, `:37`; `src/data/monsterThreat.js:28`; `src/domain/display/stateProse/defenseStateProse.js:279-284`; `src/generators/defenseGenerator.js:194-198`, `:227`, `:498`; `src/generators/stressGenerator.js:119`, `:230`, `:172-180`; `src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js:329-338`; `src/generators/structuralValidator.js:551-572` |
| L-2 | `heartland` ⇄ `settled`; `frontier`; `civilized` | `config.monsterThreat` | The producer says `heartland` and the corpus says `settled`; the desk keys on the producer token ("KEY ON THE CANONICAL PRODUCER TOKEN, NEVER ON THE CORPUS WORD"). `heartland` is LOW raider / monster exposure (the stress roll multiplies siege / monster by 0.3 and occupation by 0.4), not "the centre of a realm". `frontier` is the DEFAULT: `normalizeMonsterThreat(undefined) === 'frontier'`, so a face reading `frontier` off an unmeasured town describes a default (the desk refuses it, `measuredMonsterFamily` requires the raw value). `civilized` is forwarded UNCHANGED though not a canonical tier. [defense L-2, L-3, L-4; stressors refute §4.13] | `defenseStateProse.js:254-284`, `:271-276`, `:316-334`; `src/data/monsterThreat.js:55-56`; `stressGenerator.js:121`, `:168` |
| L-3 | `forest` / `plains` (terrain) | `config.terrainType` | Derived from the ROUTE when no override is set: `isolated → forest`, `road → plains`, `crossroads → plains`; `plains` is also the catch-all for an unknown route. WOODLAND and OPEN-GROUND are route defaults on most towns, not observations. [general L-11, C-1] | `src/generators/terrainHelpers.js:23-33` |
| L-4 | `port` (route) on non-water terrain; `port` on `riverside` | `config.tradeRouteAccess` × `terrainType` | `getTerrainType` honours ANY override, so `port` + `hills` is representable and DS-GEN-5 prints the `port` scene ("runs on the tide", "wharves") beside HIGH-GROUND. On `riverside` terrain a port is a RIVER port: `maritimeSupported = terrainType === 'coastal' \|\| (tradeRoute === 'port' && terrainType !== 'riverside')`, `portKind` may be `river`. | # | label | field | engine meaning | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## 4. ALIAS TRAPS, MERGED ACROSS THE SIX DESKS

A generic English word naming a class the engine splits into distinct rows. Every row: the word · the rows or flags it spans (with file:line) · the always-safe spelling the packets propose, where one exists · the source packets. Where two packets disagree on the safe spelling the row says so and section 5 carries it.

### 4.1 The force words

| # | word | the rows the engine splits it into | always-safe spelling | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | **the watch** | (1) `inst.hasWatch` = `['town watch','city watch','professional city watch']`, town and up only (`src/generators/priorityHelpers.js:48`); (2) `inst.hasMilitaryInst` also matches the substring `watch` (`:45`) and `inst.hasGarrison` matches `professional city watch` (`:46`), so ONE roster row sets two flags and sits in the `garrison` AND `watch` buckets (`src/domain/institutions/defenseInstitutionBuckets.js:89`, `:96`; `forceCorePoolKey` reads garrison first, `src/domain/display/stateProse/defenseStateProse.js:1269-1276`); (3) the HOLDER kind `watch` = Crime reporting / Crime response / Missing persons, backed by `Professional city watch` and `Town watch` only, and it also holds `safetyProfile`, `criminalCaptureState`, `blackMarketCapture`, the desk's one STATE ORGAN row (`src/domain/prose/holderTable.js:202-212`, `:318-324`); (4) the tier-scaled display spelling `'town watch' / 'city watch' / 'local watch'` (`src/generators/safetyProfile.js:30-35`); (5) the power generator's label ladder, where `'watch'` is a substring match that also hits the services row `Watchtower` (`src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js:74-75`, `:499-506`; `src/data/institutionServices.js:1561`); (6) the world layer puts `watch` in BOTH its `defense` and `security` classes (`src/domain/worldPulse/stressorDynamics.js:50-51`); (7) `Town watch`'s services are Night patrol, Gate duty, Crime response; `Professional city watch`'s are Law enforcement, Crime reporting, Missing persons, Escort service: "takes crime reports" is the Professional row only (`institutionServices.js:1206-1211`, `:1322-1326`); (8) the watch's PAY sits under the ORDER purse, not the military one (`src/generators/defenseGenerator.js:244-252`). ## 5. OWED TO THE CHAIR

Two lists. 5.1 is every CONDITIONAL entailment whose condition needs a WIRING DECISION (a derived slot computed from a name class, a holder-row spelling, a producer change) rather than a writer's care; a CONDITIONAL whose condition a writer can meet by reading the record as it stands (a tier, a route, a faction row, a resolved `term.type`) is left in the desk tables and not repeated here. 5.2 is every disagreement between a surveyor and its refuter, and the three places where the packets and the chair's draft law disagree. Nothing here is ruled by the folder.

## 6. THE CLOSING COUNT

Method: the desk rows are the numbered rows of each desk's main table (section 2); HOLDS and CONDITIONAL are counted as occurrences of the `(HOLDS` and `(CONDITIONAL` tags inside those rows (an ENTAILS cell may carry several items, so a row contributes as many tags as it has items; a handful of tags sit in a NOT ENTAILED or ENGINE CONTRADICTS cell where the refuter graded a contradiction CONDITIONAL, and are counted where they fall); REFUTED is the number of rows in the desk's REFUSED table, each of which may bundle several items the refuter refused together (the defense table's D-R5 is a tag rejection whose entailment survived as CONDITIONAL; the warFaith table's WF-R9 carries one REFUTED and one CONDITIONAL item). The trap counts are rows of sections 3 and 4, each row a merged, deduplicated label or word.

The six desks' census rows sum to 708, the census's whole, and their RESOLVED rows to 361, the figure ADDENDUM 5 records for the census re-take after car 8b-W (RESOLVED 340 → 361).
